There is a 697 per cent increase from 2006 to 2011. In Article 6/37 of Turkish Penal Code 5237 and international contracts, the child is described as a "not 18-year-old," but is not included in this data because sexual assault and abuse crimes are committed against those under 15/18 years of age. As a matter of fact, the number of cases brought against TCK for the rape charge also committed in article 102 was 4,419 in 2006, while the number of lawsuits filed against TCK in 2011 was 6,244 in 2011 due to the charge of sexual harassment on Article 105. 70% of these crimes are known to be intended for children. In addition, in accordance with the European Council's Convention on Sexual Protection and Protection of Children, signed on 25 November 2010 on behalf of our country on 25 October 2007, countries are obliged to take effective measures to prevent sexual exploitation and abuse of children and to protect the rights of victims exposed to sexual exploitation and abuse and develop international cooperation in this area. However, violence against children in Turkey is an obstacle to children's full use of these rights and freedoms. Children can be subjected to various forms of violence at home, school and education, working environment, homes or homes or media. In order to eliminate the phenomenon of violence, the child must be dealt with with by the extent of effective prevention, protection, intervention, treatment, healing and re-accession. To do that, you need to display the current picture of the violence against the child and develop effective strategies through this picture. In 2006, the United Nations conducted a study of the violence of children around the world. As a result of this investigation, he filed a report. The report of Violence Against the Child indicates that violence has arisen in family, school and educational institutions, boarding institutions, criminal execution institutions, work and media environments. Unfortunately, Turkey should have prepared a report by 2010 on the UN's global research that revealed the situation in Turkey, while the government still hasn't done it. However, it is possible to develop effective interventions through such research. Violence against the child is evident by various levels of data increasing day by day. At least 89 children have died in the last two years due to violence, according to the Children's Right to Life Report in Turkey. However, the 19th article of the UN Convention on Child Rights, signed by Turkey, stipulates that states must protect them against any violence that children may be subjected to. Therefore, in order for Turkey to meet this obligation effectively, an effort to address violence against the child in all its dimensions, actors, social, cultural and other reasons must be carried out within the TBM. The principle that different sexes take advantage of equal rights under the same conditions has been a basic sense of equality accepted in human rights documents. At the same time, the principle of equality is the manifestation of the principle of equality in constitutional texts in business law. In Article 23 of the 1948 Universal Declaration of Human Rights, the framework that everyone has the right to work, to choose their jobs freely, to protect them from fair and affordable work conditions and unemployment, and that everyone has the right to equal wage for equal work without distinction, and that everyone who works has the right to live a life that is worthy of the dignity of humanity and his family, and that there is a right to receive fair and affordable wage if necessary.
